1 \end{pmatrix} \textbf{n}_i = 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}nit100-1ni=0.Now, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _0,\eta _0) \in \theta $$\end{document}(ξ0,η0)∈θ, consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _0,\eta _0) + \ell _1 \textbf{n}_1 + \ell _2 \textbf{n}_2 \in \theta $$\end{document}(ξ0,η0)+ℓ1n1+ℓ2n2∈θ and we obtain by Taylor expansion:12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\quad \phi ((\xi _0,\eta _0) + \ell _1 \textbf{n}_1 + \ell _2 \textbf{n}_2) \\&= \phi (\xi _0,\eta _0) + \langle \ell _1 \textbf{n}_1 + \ell _2 \textbf{n}_2, \nabla \phi (\xi _0,\eta _0) \rangle + \frac{1}{2} \langle \ell _1 \textbf{n}_1 + \ell _2 \textbf{n}_2, \partial ^2 \phi (\ell _1 \textbf{n}_1 + \ell _2 \textbf{n}_2) \rangle \\&= \phi (\xi _0,\eta _0) + \langle \ell _1 \textbf{n}_1 + \ell _2 \textbf{n}_2, \nabla \phi (\xi _0,\eta _0) \rangle + \ell _1 \ell _2. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕ((ξ0,η0)+ℓ1n1+ℓ2n2)=ϕ(ξ0,η0)+⟨ℓ1n1+ℓ2n2,∇ϕ(ξ0,η0)⟩+12⟨ℓ1n1+ℓ2n2,∂2ϕ(ℓ1n1+ℓ2n2)⟩=ϕ(ξ0,η0)+⟨ℓ1n1+ℓ2n2,∇ϕ(ξ0,η0)⟩+ℓ1ℓ2.We obtain from the flatness condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\ell _1 \ell _2| \leqq \delta $$\end{document}|ℓ1ℓ2|≦δ. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell _{\max } = \max ( |\ell _1|,|\ell _2| )$$\end{document}ℓmax=max(|ℓ1|,|ℓ2|), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell _{\min } = \min ( |\ell _1|, |\ell _2|)$$\end{document}ℓmin=min(|ℓ1|,|ℓ2|). We reverse the continuous approximation and the scaling to find the discrete decoupling inequality:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \big \Vert e^{it(\partial _1^2 - \partial _2^2)} P_N f \big \Vert _{L^4_{t,x,y}([0,1]; \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})} \lesssim _\varepsilon \delta ^{-\varepsilon } \big ( \sum _{\theta \in \Theta _{\delta }} \big \Vert e^{it (\partial _1^2 - \partial _2^2)} P_{N \theta } f \big \Vert ^2_{L^4_{t,x,y}([0,1];\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})} \big )^{\frac{1}{2}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖eit(∂12-∂22)PNf‖Lt,x,y4([0,1];R×T)≲εδ-ε(∑θ∈Θδ‖eit(∂12-∂22)PNθf‖Lt,x,y4([0,1];R×T)2)12,with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N \theta $$\end{document}Nθ denoting the N-dilation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta \in \Theta _{\delta }$$\end{document}θ∈Θδ. Following the general argument from the previous subsection we can integrate out the time evolution and the final ingredient is an estimate of the product measure \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {meas}_{\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {Z}}( N \theta )$$\end{document}measR×Z(Nθ). Write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N\theta \cap (\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {Z}) = \bigcup _{\eta \in \pi _{\eta }(N \theta )} I_{\eta }$$\end{document}Nθ∩(R×Z)=⋃η∈πη(Nθ)Iη. We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \# \, \{ \, \eta \in \pi _{\eta }(N \theta ) \, \} \, \lesssim \langle N \ell _{\max } \rangle , \quad |I_{\eta }| \lesssim N \ell _{\min }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}#{η∈πη(Nθ)}≲⟨Nℓmax⟩,|Iη|≲Nℓmin,and consequently \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {meas}_{\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {Z}}( N \theta ) \lesssim 1$$\end{document}measR×Z(Nθ)≲1. With this, the proof is concluded by Bernstein’s inequality\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert P_{N \theta } f \Vert _{L^4_{xy}(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})} \lesssim \Vert P_{N \theta } f \Vert _{L^2_{xy}(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖PNθf‖Lxy4(R×T)≲‖PNθf‖Lxy2(R×T)and logarithmic overlap of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta \in \Theta _{\delta }$$\end{document}θ∈Θδ. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 3.4

On the domain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {D}= \mathbb {T}^2$$\end{document}D=T2 the flat sets remain the same, but the measure changes. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ A' = \{ (\eta ,\eta ) \in \mathbb {Z}^2: \eta \in [N/2,N] \cap \mathbb {Z}\} $$\end{document}A′={(η,η)∈Z2:η∈[N/2,N]∩Z} we find \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {meas}_{\mathbb {Z}^2} (A') \sim N$$\end{document}measZ2(A′)∼N, which matches the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^4$$\end{document}L4-Strichartz estimate with derivative loss.

Decoupling and Strichartz estimates for generalized KP-II dispersion relation

In this section we apply the general argument outlined in the preceding section to fractional KP-II equations. We note that the dispersion relation\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \omega _\alpha (\xi ,\eta ) = \xi |\xi |^\alpha - \frac{\eta ^2}{\xi } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ωα(ξ,η)=ξ|ξ|α-η2ξis hyperbolic with principal curvatures having modulus essentially 1.

Lemma 3.5

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in [1,2]$$\end{document}α∈[1,2]. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_\alpha = \{(\xi ,\eta ,\omega _\alpha (\xi ,\eta )): \xi \sim 1, \, |\eta | \lesssim 1 \}$$\end{document}Mα={(ξ,η,ωα(ξ,η)):ξ∼1,|η|≲1} is a non-elliptic hypersurface with principle curvatures \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _1$$\end{document}λ1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _2$$\end{document}λ2 satisyfing\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |\lambda _1| \sim |\lambda _2| \sim 1, \text { and } \text {sgn}(\lambda _1 \lambda _2) = - 1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|λ1|∼|λ2|∼1,and sgn(λ1λ2)=-1.

Proof

We compute\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial ^2 \omega _\alpha = \begin{pmatrix} (\alpha +1) \alpha \xi ^{\alpha -1} - 2 \eta ^2 / \xi ^3 & 2 \eta /\xi ^2 \\ 2 \eta /\xi ^2 & - 2/\xi \end{pmatrix} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂2ωα=(α+1)αξα-1-2η2/ξ32η/ξ22η/ξ2-2/ξ.Clearly, it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \det (\partial ^2 \omega _\alpha ) = -2(\alpha +1)\alpha \xi ^{\alpha -2} \Rightarrow \det (\partial ^2 \omega _\alpha ) \sim -1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}det(∂2ωα)=-2(α+1)αξα-2⇒det(∂2ωα)∼-1.Moreover, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\text {tr}(\partial ^2 \omega _\alpha ) | \lesssim 1$$\end{document}|tr(∂2ωα)|≲1. This shows that the eigenvalues of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial ^2 \omega _\alpha $$\end{document}∂2ωα are of opposite sign and have modulus comparable to 1. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

By the above considerations we need to analyze the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat sets for\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {M}_{\alpha } = \{ (\xi ,\eta ,\omega _{\alpha }(\xi ,\eta )) : |\xi | \sim 1, \; |\eta | \lesssim 1 \}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Mα={(ξ,η,ωα(ξ,η)):|ξ|∼1,|η|≲1}.The case of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha =2$$\end{document}α=2 was detailed in [28]:

Proposition 3.6

( [28, Proposition 3.4, Remark 3.5]) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A \subseteq \mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}A⊆R2 be a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat set for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_2$$\end{document}M2. Then A is contained in a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}$$\end{document}δ13-ball. Moreover, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _0,\eta _0) \in A$$\end{document}(ξ0,η0)∈A, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\eta -\eta _0| \lesssim \delta ^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}|η-η0|≲δ12 for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _0,\eta ) \in A$$\end{document}(ξ0,η)∈A.

Like in case of the KP-II dispersion relation we show the following:

Proposition 3.7

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in [1,2]$$\end{document}α∈[1,2] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta $$\end{document}θ a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat rectangle for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_\alpha $$\end{document}Mα. We have the following properties: (A)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta $$\end{document}θ is contained in a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}$$\end{document}δ13-ball.(B)For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _0,\eta _0), (\xi _0,\eta ) \in \theta $$\end{document}(ξ0,η0),(ξ0,η)∈θ it holds \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\eta - \eta _0| \lesssim \delta ^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}|η-η0|≲δ12.

(A)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta $$\end{document}θ is contained in a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}$$\end{document}δ13-ball.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta $$\end{document}θ is contained in a \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}$$\end{document}δ13-ball.

(B)For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _0,\eta _0), (\xi _0,\eta ) \in \theta $$\end{document}(ξ0,η0),(ξ0,η)∈θ it holds \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\eta - \eta _0| \lesssim \delta ^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}|η-η0|≲δ12.

For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _0,\eta _0), (\xi _0,\eta ) \in \theta $$\end{document}(ξ0,η0),(ξ0,η)∈θ it holds \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\eta - \eta _0| \lesssim \delta ^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}|η-η0|≲δ12.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _0,\eta _0) \in \theta $$\end{document}(ξ0,η0)∈θ and consider a curve\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \gamma (t) = (\xi _0,\eta _0) + t (\xi ',\eta ') \end{aligned}$$\end{document}γ(t)=(ξ0,η0)+t(ξ′,η′)with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|(\xi ',\eta ')| = 1$$\end{document}|(ξ′,η′)|=1. For (A) it suffices to show that13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \omega _\alpha (\gamma (t)) = a_0 + a_1 t + a_2 t^2 + a_3 t^3 + E(t) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ωα(γ(t))=a0+a1t+a2t2+a3t3+E(t)with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|a_2|+|a_3| \gtrsim 1$$\end{document}|a2|+|a3|≳1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|E(t)| \leqq c t^4$$\end{document}|E(t)|≦ct4. We have14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \omega _\alpha (\xi _0+t\xi ',\eta _0+t\eta ') = (\xi _0+t\xi ')^{\alpha +1} - \frac{(\eta _0+t\eta ')^2}{\xi _0 + t \xi '}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ωα(ξ0+tξ′,η0+tη′)=(ξ0+tξ′)α+1-(η0+tη′)2ξ0+tξ′.First, we handle the simple case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\xi '| \ll 1$$\end{document}|ξ′|≪1, hence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\eta '| \gtrsim 1$$\end{document}|η′|≳1. We find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} (\xi _0+t\xi ')^{\alpha +1} - \frac{(\eta _0+t \eta ')^2}{\xi _0 + t \xi '}&= \xi _0^{\alpha +1} + \mathcal {O}(\xi ') - \frac{\eta _0^2 + 2 t \eta _0 \eta ' + t^2 (\eta ')^2}{\xi _0}(1+ \mathcal {O}(\xi ')) \\&= \xi _0^{\alpha +1} - \frac{\eta _0^2}{\xi _0} + \frac{2 t \eta _0 }{\xi _0} - \frac{t^2 (\eta ')^2}{\xi _0} + \mathcal {O}(\xi '). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(ξ0+tξ′)α+1-(η0+tη′)2ξ0+tξ′=ξ0α+1+O(ξ′)-η02+2tη0η′+t2(η′)2ξ0(1+O(ξ′))=ξ0α+1-η02ξ0+2tη0ξ0-t2(η′)2ξ0+O(ξ′).This gives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a_2 = - \frac{(\eta ')^2}{\xi _0} + \mathcal {O}(\xi ')$$\end{document}a2=-(η′)2ξ0+O(ξ′) and hence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|a_2| \gtrsim 1$$\end{document}|a2|≳1. Moreover, this shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta $$\end{document}θ is extended in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η-direction with length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}δ12, which yields (B).

We turn to the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\xi '| \gtrsim 1$$\end{document}|ξ′|≳1. We can now apply the Galilean invariance:15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \omega _{\alpha }(\xi ,\eta + A \xi ) = \omega _\alpha (\xi ,\eta ) - 2 A \eta + A^2 \xi \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ωα(ξ,η+Aξ)=ωα(ξ,η)-2Aη+A2ξwith \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi = \xi _0 + t \xi '$$\end{document}ξ=ξ0+tξ′, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta = \eta _0 + t \eta '$$\end{document}η=η0+tη′, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A = - \eta ' / \xi '$$\end{document}A=-η′/ξ′. It follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \omega _\alpha (\xi ,\eta ) = \omega _\alpha (\xi ,\eta +A \xi ) + 2 A \eta - A^2 \xi = \omega _\alpha (\xi ,\eta - \frac{\eta '}{\xi '} \xi ) + \text {Lin}(t). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ωα(ξ,η)=ωα(ξ,η+Aξ)+2Aη-A2ξ=ωα(ξ,η-η′ξ′ξ)+Lin(t).The linear term can be disregarded. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\eta } = \eta _0 - \frac{\eta '}{\xi '} \xi _0$$\end{document}η¯=η0-η′ξ′ξ0. We find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} (\xi _0 + t \xi ')^{\alpha +1}&= \xi _0^{\alpha +1} + (\alpha +1) t \xi ' \xi _0^{\alpha } + (\alpha + 1) \alpha \xi _0^{\alpha -1} \frac{(t\xi ')^2}{2} \\&\quad + (\alpha +1) \alpha (\alpha -1) \xi _0^{\alpha -2} \frac{(t \xi ')^3}{3!} + \mathcal {O}(t^4) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(ξ0+tξ′)α+1=ξ0α+1+(α+1)tξ′ξ0α+(α+1)αξ0α-1(tξ′)22+(α+1)α(α-1)ξ0α-2(tξ′)33!+O(t4)and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\bar{\eta }^2}{\xi _0 + t \xi '} = \frac{\bar{\eta }^2}{\xi _0} ( 1- \frac{t \xi '}{\xi _0} + \frac{t^2 (\xi ')^2}{\xi _0^2} - \frac{t^3 (\xi ')^3}{\xi _0^3} + \mathcal {O}(t^4)). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}η¯2ξ0+tξ′=η¯2ξ0(1-tξ′ξ0+t2(ξ′)2ξ02-t3(ξ′)3ξ03+O(t4)).Taking the above identities together, we find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \omega _\alpha (\xi ,\eta - \frac{\eta '}{\xi '} \xi )&= \omega _\alpha (\xi ,\bar{\eta }) = (\xi _0 + t \xi ')^{\alpha +1} - \frac{\bar{\eta }^2}{\xi _0 + t \xi '} \\ \\&= \xi _0^{\alpha +1} + \text {Lin}(t) + t^2\big ( \frac{(\alpha +1) \alpha \xi _0^{\alpha -1}}{2} - \frac{\bar{\eta }^2}{\xi _0^3} \big ) \\&\quad + t^3 (\xi ')^3 \big ( \frac{(\alpha +1) \alpha (\alpha -1) \xi _0^{\alpha -2}}{3!} + \frac{\bar{\eta }^2}{\xi _0^4} \big ) + \mathcal {O}(t^4). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ωα(ξ,η-η′ξ′ξ)=ωα(ξ,η¯)=(ξ0+tξ′)α+1-η¯2ξ0+tξ′=ξ0α+1+Lin(t)+t2((α+1)αξ0α-12-η¯2ξ03)+t3(ξ′)3((α+1)α(α-1)ξ0α-23!+η¯2ξ04)+O(t4).We see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a_2$$\end{document}a2 can vanish in the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\bar{\eta }| \sim 1$$\end{document}|η¯|∼1, which necessitates \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\eta _0| \sim 1$$\end{document}|η0|∼1 since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _0 \sim 1$$\end{document}ξ0∼1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\eta '/\xi '| \ll 1$$\end{document}|η′/ξ′|≪1. For (A) it suffices to observe that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|a_3| \gtrsim 1$$\end{document}|a3|≳1, which yields a direction \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi ',\eta ')$$\end{document}(ξ′,η′), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\xi '| \gtrsim 1$$\end{document}|ξ′|≳1, into which \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta $$\end{document}θ is extended with length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lesssim \delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}$$\end{document}≲δ13. The proof is complete.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

To obtain a more precise estimate for the measure of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat sets, we recall that these take the form of parallelograms and we observe that in case of normalized derivatives these have area \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sim \delta $$\end{document}∼δ as follows basically from the same argument as in (12). Indeed, in the case of perturbed hyperboloids the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat sets S constructed in [21, Section 2.1] are comparable to rectangles with one side pointing into a null direction.

Note that in the more general case of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_\alpha $$\end{document}Mα, we have indeed uniformly bounded derivatives in the region \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{ \xi \sim 1, \; |\eta | \lesssim 1 \}$$\end{document}{ξ∼1,|η|≲1}. On the other hand, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha =2$$\end{document}α=2 we find \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta = N^{-3}$$\end{document}δ=N-3 and a flat set with area larger than \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{1-\varepsilon }$$\end{document}δ1-ε for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon > 0$$\end{document}ε>0 would give a counterexample to the Strichartz estimate on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}R2 without derivative loss. Recall the following due to Saut [53]:16\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert S_\alpha (t) f \Vert _{L^4_{t,x,y}([0,1];\mathbb {R}^2)} \lesssim \Vert f \Vert _{L^2(\mathbb {R}^2)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Sα(t)f‖Lt,x,y4([0,1];R2)≲‖f‖L2(R2).The reason is that after anisotropic dilation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \rightarrow N \xi $$\end{document}ξ→Nξ, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta \rightarrow N^2 \eta $$\end{document}η→N2η (see below), the area of the rescaled flat set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _{N,N^2}$$\end{document}θN,N2 would be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gtrsim N^{\varepsilon }$$\end{document}≳Nε. We could take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{f} = \chi _{\theta _{N,N^2}}$$\end{document}f^=χθN,N2 and since there is no time oscillation, we obtain from sharpness of Bernstein’s inequality\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert S_\alpha (t) f \Vert _{L^4_{t,x,y}([0,1];\mathbb {R}^2 )} \sim \Vert f \Vert _{L^4_{x,y}(\mathbb {R}^2)} \sim N^{\frac{\varepsilon }{4}} \Vert f \Vert _{L^2_{x,y}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Sα(t)f‖Lt,x,y4([0,1];R2)∼‖f‖Lx,y4(R2)∼Nε4‖f‖Lx,y2.This clearly contradicts (16).

The case of perturbed hyperboloids is recovered for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_\alpha $$\end{document}Mα by mild anisotropic dilation and rotation as pointed out in Lemma 3.5. Hence, the flat sets for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_\alpha $$\end{document}Mα can be chosen as parallelograms with angle \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gtrsim 1$$\end{document}≳1 and area \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sim \delta $$\end{document}∼δ. That having been said, we have the following characterization for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat sets:

Proposition 3.8

Up to Galilean transform the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat parallelograms for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_\alpha $$\end{document}Mα, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in [1,2]$$\end{document}α∈[1,2] are contained in rectangles of size \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\ell _1,\ell _2)$$\end{document}(ℓ1,ℓ2), which are extended by length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell _i$$\end{document}ℓi into \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e_i$$\end{document}ei-direction, and satisfy the bounds \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{2}} \lesssim \ell _1 \lesssim \delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}$$\end{document}δ12≲ℓ1≲δ13, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell _2 = \delta \ell _1^{-1}$$\end{document}ℓ2=δℓ1-1.

Proof

The null directions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi ',\eta ')$$\end{document}(ξ′,η′) are the ones annihilating the quadratic term in (13) and for this reason satisfy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\xi '| \gtrsim 1$$\end{document}|ξ′|≳1. So, carrying out the Galilean transform, these can be transformed to the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e_1$$\end{document}e1-direction. Consequently, the first null direction of the transformed parallelogram is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e_1$$\end{document}e1 and the length satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell _1 \lesssim \delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}$$\end{document}ℓ1≲δ13.

In the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e_2$$\end{document}e2-direction, by Proposition 3.7 we know that the length is at most \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}δ12. This points out that the extension \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell _2$$\end{document}ℓ2 in the second direction is at most \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}δ12. By the area constraint \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell _1 \ell _2 \lesssim \delta $$\end{document}ℓ1ℓ2≲δ, we have for some fixed \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B \gg 1$$\end{document}B≫1:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \delta ^{\frac{1}{2}} \lesssim \ell _1 \lesssim \delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}, \quad \ell _2 = \delta \ell _1^{-1} \in (B^{-1} \delta ^{\frac{2}{3}},B \delta ^{\frac{1}{2}} ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}δ12≲ℓ1≲δ13,ℓ2=δℓ1-1∈(B-1δ23,Bδ12).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We intend to show Strichartz estimates in the Fourier region:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} A_{N,N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}} = \{ (\xi ,\eta ) \in \mathbb {R}^2 : |\xi | \sim N, \; |\eta | \lesssim N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1} \}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}AN,Nα2+1={(ξ,η)∈R2:|ξ|∼N,|η|≲Nα2+1}.Recall that the scaling is given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \xi \rightarrow \xi /N, \quad \eta \rightarrow \eta /N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ξ→ξ/N,η→η/Nα2+1and with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t \rightarrow N^{\alpha +1} t$$\end{document}t→Nα+1t, on the unit time scale we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta = N^{-(\alpha +1)}$$\end{document}δ=N-(α+1).

Consider the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{|\eta | \lesssim N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1} \}$$\end{document}{|η|≲Nα2+1}. After rescaling the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat set, we find sets of length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\ell _1' \in (N^{\frac{1-\alpha }{2}},N^{\frac{2-\alpha }{3}})$$\end{document}ℓ1′∈(N1-α2,N2-α3) in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi $$\end{document}ξ-frequencies, and length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^{-\frac{\alpha }{2}} \ell _1^{-1} = N^{1-\frac{\alpha }{2}} (\ell _1')^{-1}$$\end{document}N-α2ℓ1-1=N1-α2(ℓ1′)-1 in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η-frequencies.Fig. 1Anisotropic scaling transforms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat sets

Anisotropic scaling transforms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat sets

By following the strategy of applying decoupling outlined in Section 3.1, we can show the following Strichartz estimates. Here we additionally cover preimages of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_{N,N^2}$$\end{document}AN,N2 under the Galilean transform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta \rightarrow \eta - A \xi $$\end{document}η→η-Aξ, which will be required for the nonlinear analysis.

Proposition 3.9

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in [1,2]$$\end{document}α∈[1,2]. For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N \in 2^{\mathbb {N}_0}$$\end{document}N∈2N0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A\in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}A∈R, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in L^2(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})$$\end{document}f∈L2(R×T) with the property\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \text {supp}(\hat{f}) \subseteq \{ (\xi ,\eta ) \in \mathbb {R}^2 : |\xi | \sim N, \; \big | \frac{\eta }{\xi } - A \big | \lesssim N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}} \}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supp(f^)⊆{(ξ,η)∈R2:|ξ|∼N,|ηξ-A|≲Nα2}.we have the following estimate:17\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert S_{\alpha }(t) f \Vert _{L^4_{t}([0,1],L^4_{xy}(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T}))} \lesssim _\varepsilon N^{\frac{2-\alpha }{8}+ \varepsilon } \Vert f \Vert _{L^2(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Sα(t)f‖Lt4([0,1],Lxy4(R×T))≲εN2-α8+ε‖f‖L2(R×T).

Proof

Moreover, we can suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{ \xi \sim N \}$$\end{document}{ξ∼N} in the Fourier support of f. Proof of (17) in case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{ |\eta | \lesssim N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1} \}$$\end{document}{|η|≲Nα2+1}.
