\end{aligned}$$\end{document}#{η:(ξ,η)∈θN,Nα2+1}∼N1-α2(ℓ1′)-1≳1,and readily,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \text {meas}_{\mathbb {R}^2}(\theta _{N,N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}}) \lesssim N^{1-\frac{\alpha }{2}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}measR2(θN,Nα2+1)≲N1-α2.For this reason, an application of Bernstein’s inequality yields20\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert f_{\theta _{N,N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}}} \Vert _{L^4_{x,y}(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})} \lesssim N^{\frac{2-\alpha }{8}} \Vert f_{\theta _{N,N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}}} \Vert _{L^2_{x,y}(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖fθN,Nα2+1‖Lx,y4(R×T)≲N2-α8‖fθN,Nα2+1‖Lx,y2(R×T).Proof of (17) in the general case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{ \big | \frac{\eta }{\xi } - A \big | \ll N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}} \}$$\end{document}{|ηξ-A|≪Nα2} for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A \gtrsim N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}$$\end{document}A≳Nα2. Note that the Galilean transform \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G: (\xi ,\eta ) \mapsto (\xi '=\xi ,\eta '=\eta + A\xi )$$\end{document}G:(ξ,η)↦(ξ′=ξ,η′=η+Aξ) maps the region \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\big | \frac{\eta }{\xi }-A \big | \ll N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}$$\end{document}|ηξ-A|≪Nα2 to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\big | \frac{\eta '}{\xi '} \big | \ll N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}$$\end{document}|η′ξ′|≪Nα2. The idea is to apply decoupling to the transformed frequencies, which will give a decomposition into flat sets with respect to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi ',\eta ')$$\end{document}(ξ′,η′). Then we reverse the Galilean transform and we need to estimate the measure of the preimage \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G^{-1} \theta _{N,N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}}$$\end{document}G-1θN,Nα2+1 under Galilean transform. We argue like above with the comparability\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \text {meas}_{\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {Z}} (\theta _{N,N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}}) \sim \text {meas}_{\mathbb {R}^2} (\theta _{N,N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}}). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}measR×Z(θN,Nα2+1)∼measR2(θN,Nα2+1).This allows us to dispense the Galilean transform, which clearly does not change the Lebesgue measure. We recover (20) independently of A, which proves (17) in the general case. The proof is complete.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 3.10

The derivative loss for the Strichartz estimates are sharp up to endpoints. This follows from the comparison with the sharp estimates in the Euclidean case, also for fractional dispersion relation. Indeed, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in [1,2]$$\end{document}α∈[1,2], Hadac [22, Theorem 3.1] proved the scaling critical estimate\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert S_{\alpha }(t) f \Vert _{L_t^4([0,1],L^4_{xy}(\mathbb {R}^2))} \lesssim \Vert |D_x|^{\frac{2-\alpha }{8}} f \Vert _{L^2_{xy}(\mathbb {R}^2)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Sα(t)f‖Lt4([0,1],Lxy4(R2))≲‖|Dx|2-α8f‖Lxy2(R2).

Strichartz estimates for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{ |\xi | \sim N, \; |\frac{\eta }{\xi } - A| \lesssim N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+\gamma } \}$$\end{document}{|ξ|∼N,|ηξ-A|≲Nα2+γ} for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma \in (0,1]$$\end{document}γ∈(0,1]

Decompose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+\gamma }]$$\end{document}[0,Nα2+γ] into intervals of length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}$$\end{document}Nα2. Then, after applying a Galilean transform, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\bar{\eta }/\xi |$$\end{document}|η¯/ξ| is contained in an interval of length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}$$\end{document}Nα2, that is, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\eta }$$\end{document}η¯ is contained in an interval of length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}$$\end{document}Nα2+1.

After shifting the range of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta '$$\end{document}η′-frequencies to the origin, the transformed phase function is defined on:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \{ |\xi | \sim N, \quad |\eta '| \lesssim N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1} \}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}{|ξ|∼N,|η′|≲Nα2+1}.Rescaling \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \rightarrow \xi / N = \xi '$$\end{document}ξ→ξ/N=ξ′, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta ' \rightarrow \eta '/ N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1} $$\end{document}η′→η′/Nα2+1 yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \omega _\alpha (\xi ',\eta ') = \xi ' |\xi '|^{\alpha } - \frac{(\eta ' - \eta ^*)^2}{\xi '}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ωα(ξ′,η′)=ξ′|ξ′|α-(η′-η∗)2ξ′.Note that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\eta ^*| \lesssim N^\gamma $$\end{document}|η∗|≲Nγ. To obtain a phase function with uniform bounded derivatives we subdivide \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi '$$\end{document}ξ′ into intervals of length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^{-\gamma }$$\end{document}N-γ. The resulting phase function takes the form21\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \bar{\omega }_\alpha (\xi ',\eta ') = (\kappa ^{-1} \xi ' + \xi ^*)^{\alpha +1} - \frac{(\eta ' - \eta ^*)^2}{\xi ' \kappa ^{-1} + \xi ^*} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ω¯α(ξ′,η′)=(κ-1ξ′+ξ∗)α+1-(η′-η∗)2ξ′κ-1+ξ∗for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\xi '| \lesssim 1$$\end{document}|ξ′|≲1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa = |\eta ^*| \lesssim N^{\gamma }$$\end{document}κ=|η∗|≲Nγ, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _0' \sim 1$$\end{document}ξ0′∼1. In the first step we obtain the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat sets for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\omega }_\alpha $$\end{document}ω¯α, then we need to reverse the Galilean transform and estimate the measure of the preimages.

We have the following characterization of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat sets of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\omega }_{\alpha }$$\end{document}ω¯α:

Proposition 3.11

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _0,\eta _0,\xi ,\eta $$\end{document}ξ0,η0,ξ,η be like above, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in [1,2]$$\end{document}α∈[1,2]. Then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta $$\end{document}δ-flat sets of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\omega }_\alpha $$\end{document}ω¯α defined in (21) are contained in rectangles of dimensions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\ell _1,\ell _2)$$\end{document}(ℓ1,ℓ2) of length\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \delta ^{\frac{1}{2}} \lesssim \ell _1 \lesssim \kappa \delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}, \quad \ell _2 = \delta \ell _1^{-1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}δ12≲ℓ1≲κδ13,ℓ2=δℓ1-1.

Proof

This can be proved following along the above lines. We shall be brief. The maximum size of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}δ12 into \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e_2$$\end{document}e2-direction can be found exactly like above.

Next, we consider a curve parametrized by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi ,\eta ) = (\xi _0 + t \xi ', \eta _0 + t \eta ')$$\end{document}(ξ,η)=(ξ0+tξ′,η0+tη′) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\xi '| \gtrsim 1$$\end{document}|ξ′|≳1. Let\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \bar{\xi } = \kappa ^{-1} (\xi _0 + t \xi ') + \xi ^*, \quad \bar{\eta } = \eta _0 - t \eta ' - \eta ^*. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ξ¯=κ-1(ξ0+tξ′)+ξ∗,η¯=η0-tη′-η∗.We consider the Galilean transformation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\eta } \rightarrow \bar{\eta } + A \bar{\xi }$$\end{document}η¯→η¯+Aξ¯ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A= \frac{\kappa \eta '}{\xi '}$$\end{document}A=κη′ξ′, which leads us to consider\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\quad (\kappa ^{-1} (\xi _0 + t \xi ') + \xi ^*)^{\alpha +1} - \frac{(\bar{\eta } + A \bar{\xi })^2}{\bar{\xi }} \\&= (\kappa ^{-1} (\xi _0 + t \xi ') + \xi ^*)^{\alpha +1} - \frac{\bar{\eta }^2}{\bar{\xi }} + \text {Lin}(t) \\&= (\kappa ^{-1} (\xi _0 + t \xi ') + \xi ^*)^{\alpha +1} - \frac{(\eta _0 + \frac{\eta ' \xi _0}{\xi '} + \frac{\kappa \eta ' \xi ^*}{\xi '} - \eta ^*)^2}{\kappa ^{-1} \xi + \xi ^*} + \text {Lin}(t) \\&= (\kappa ^{-1} (\xi _0 + t \xi ') + \xi ^*)^{\alpha +1} - \frac{(\bar{\eta }_0 - \eta ^*)^2}{\kappa ^{-1}\xi + \xi ^*} + \text {Lin}(t). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(κ-1(ξ0+tξ′)+ξ∗)α+1-(η¯+Aξ¯)2ξ¯=(κ-1(ξ0+tξ′)+ξ∗)α+1-η¯2ξ¯+Lin(t)=(κ-1(ξ0+tξ′)+ξ∗)α+1-(η0+η′ξ0ξ′+κη′ξ∗ξ′-η∗)2κ-1ξ+ξ∗+Lin(t)=(κ-1(ξ0+tξ′)+ξ∗)α+1-(η¯0-η∗)2κ-1ξ+ξ∗+Lin(t).Next, the expression is expanded in t. Note that the derivatives are uniformly bounded by means of the renormalization in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi $$\end{document}ξ. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\xi }_0 = \xi ^* + \kappa ^{-1} \xi _0$$\end{document}ξ¯0=ξ∗+κ-1ξ0 for brevity.

We find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} (\bar{\xi }_0 + \kappa ^{-1} t \xi ')^{\alpha +1}&= \bar{\xi }_0^{\alpha +1} + (\alpha +1) \bar{\xi }_0^{\alpha } \kappa ^{-1} t \xi ' + (\alpha +1) \alpha \bar{\xi }_0^{\alpha -1} \frac{(\kappa ^{-1} t \xi ')^2}{2} \\&\quad + (\alpha +1) \alpha (\alpha -1) \bar{\xi }_0^{\alpha -2} \frac{(\kappa ^{-1} t \xi ')^3}{3!} + \mathcal {O}(t^4). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(ξ¯0+κ-1tξ′)α+1=ξ¯0α+1+(α+1)ξ¯0ακ-1tξ′+(α+1)αξ¯0α-1(κ-1tξ′)22+(α+1)α(α-1)ξ¯0α-2(κ-1tξ′)33!+O(t4).Secondly,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{(\bar{\eta }_0 - \eta ^*)^2}{\bar{\xi }_0(1+ \frac{t \kappa ^{-1} \xi '}{\xi _0})} = \frac{(\bar{\eta }_0 - \eta ^*)^2}{\bar{\xi }_0} (1 - \frac{t \kappa ^{-1} \xi '}{\bar{\xi }_0} + \frac{t^2 \kappa ^{-2} (\xi ')^2}{\bar{\xi }_0^2} - \frac{t^3 \kappa ^{-3} (\xi ')^3}{\bar{\xi }_0^3} + \mathcal {O}(t^4)). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(η¯0-η∗)2ξ¯0(1+tκ-1ξ′ξ0)=(η¯0-η∗)2ξ¯0(1-tκ-1ξ′ξ¯0+t2κ-2(ξ′)2ξ¯02-t3κ-3(ξ′)3ξ¯03+O(t4)).To summarize, we find that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\quad (\bar{\xi }_0 + \kappa ^{-1} t \xi ')^{\alpha +1} - \frac{(\bar{\eta }_0 - \eta ^*)^2}{\bar{\xi }_0 ( 1+ \frac{t \kappa ^{-1} \xi '}{\xi _0}} ) \\&= \text {Lin}(t) + \big [ (\alpha +1) \alpha \bar{\xi }_0^{\alpha -1} \frac{\kappa ^{-2} t^2 (\xi ')^2}{2} - \frac{(\bar{\eta }_0 -\eta ^*)^2}{\bar{\xi }_0} \frac{t^2 \kappa ^{-2} (\xi ')^2}{\bar{\xi }_0^3} \big ] \\&\quad + \big [ (\alpha +1) \alpha (\alpha -1) \bar{\xi }_0^{\alpha -2} \frac{(\kappa ^{-1} t \xi ')^3}{3!} + \frac{(\bar{\eta }_0 -\eta ^*)^2}{\bar{\xi }_0} \frac{t^3 \kappa ^{-3} (\xi ')^3}{\bar{\xi }_0^3} \big ] + \mathcal {O}(t^4). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(ξ¯0+κ-1tξ′)α+1-(η¯0-η∗)2ξ¯0(1+tκ-1ξ′ξ0)=Lin(t)+[(α+1)αξ¯0α-1κ-2t2(ξ′)22-(η¯0-η∗)2ξ¯0t2κ-2(ξ′)2ξ¯03]+[(α+1)α(α-1)ξ¯0α-2(κ-1tξ′)33!+(η¯0-η∗)2ξ¯0t3κ-3(ξ′)3ξ¯03]+O(t4).Like above, the quadratic term may vanish, but the cubic term is always \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gtrsim \kappa ^{-3}$$\end{document}≳κ-3. This shows that the maximal size of flat parallelograms in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e_1$$\end{document}e1-direction is at most \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa \delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}$$\end{document}κδ13. By the maximum length of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta ^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}δ12 into \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e_2$$\end{document}e2-direction we have found\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \delta ^{\frac{1}{2}} \lesssim \ell _1 \lesssim \kappa \delta ^{\frac{1}{3}}, \quad \ell _2 = \delta \ell _1^{-1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}δ12≲ℓ1≲κδ13,ℓ2=δℓ1-1.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We are ready to show the following Strichartz estimates:

Proposition 3.12

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f: \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T}\rightarrow \mathbb {C}$$\end{document}f:R×T→C with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {supp}(\hat{f}) \subseteq \{(\xi ,\eta ) \in \mathbb {R}^2: |\xi | \sim N, \xi \in I, |I| \sim N/k, |\frac{\eta }{\xi } - A| \in [k N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}, (k+1) N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}] \}$$\end{document}supp(f^)⊆{(ξ,η)∈R2:|ξ|∼N,ξ∈I,|I|∼N/k,|ηξ-A|∈[kNα2,(k+1)Nα2]} with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \ll k \lesssim N$$\end{document}1≪k≲N. Then the following estimate holds:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert S_\alpha (t) f \Vert _{L_{t,x,y}^4([0,1], \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})} \lesssim _\varepsilon (N^{\frac{2-\alpha }{12}+\varepsilon } k^{\frac{1}{12}} \vee N^{\frac{2-\alpha }{8}+\varepsilon }) \Vert f \Vert _{L^2(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Sα(t)f‖Lt,x,y4([0,1],R×T)≲ε(N2-α12+εk112∨N2-α8+ε)‖f‖L2(R×T).

Proof

Like in the proof of Proposition 3.9, we first deal with the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A \lesssim N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}$$\end{document}A≲Nα2. We elaborate on the modifications in the general case below.

Use the scaling\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} t \rightarrow N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1} t, \quad x \rightarrow N x, \quad y \rightarrow N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1} y \end{aligned}$$\end{document}t→Nα2+1t,x→Nx,y→Nα2+1yand periodicity in y to find\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\Vert S_\alpha (t) f \Vert ^4_{L_{t,x,y}^4([0,1]; \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})} \\&\quad = N^{-(\alpha +1)} N^{-\frac{\alpha }{2}} N^{-2} \\&\qquad \times \big \Vert \int _{\xi ' \in I'} \sum _{\eta ' \in \mathbb {Z}/ N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}} e^{i(x' \xi ' + y' \eta ' + t'((\xi ')^{\alpha +1} - \frac{(\eta '+\eta _0')^2}{\xi '})} \hat{f}(\xi ',\eta ') \big \Vert ^4_{L^4_{t',x',y'}([0,N^{\alpha +1}] \times N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1} \mathbb {T})} \\&\quad \lesssim N^{-(\alpha +1)} N^{-\frac{\alpha }{2}} N^{-2} N^{-\frac{\alpha }{2}} \\&\qquad \times \big \Vert \int _{\xi ' \in I'} \sum _{\eta ' \in \mathbb {Z}/ N^{\frac{\alpha }{2}+1}} e^{i(x' \xi ' + y' \eta ' + t'((\xi ')^{\alpha +1} - \frac{(\eta '+\eta _0')^2}{\xi '})} \hat{f}(\xi ',\eta ') \big \Vert ^4_{L^4_{t',x',y'}([0,N^{\alpha +1}] \times \mathbb {R}\times N^{\alpha +1} \mathbb {T})}. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Sα(t)f‖Lt,x,y4([0,1];R×T)4=N-(α+1)N-α2N-2×‖∫ξ′∈I′∑η′∈Z/Nα2+1ei(x′ξ′+y′η′+t′((ξ′)α+1-(η′+η0′)2ξ′)f^(ξ′,η′)‖Lt′,x′,y′4([0,Nα+1]×Nα2+1T)4≲N-(α+1)N-α2N-2N-α2×‖∫ξ′∈I′∑η′∈Z/Nα2+1ei(x′ξ′+y′η′+t′((ξ′)α+1-(η′+η0′)2ξ′)f^(ξ′,η′)‖Lt′,x′,y′4([0,Nα+1]×R×Nα+1T)4.We have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta _0 \in [k-2,k+2]$$\end{document}η0∈[k-2,k+2] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi ' \in I'$$\end{document}ξ′∈I′ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|I'| \sim 1/k \sim 1/\eta _0$$\end{document}|I′|∼1/k∼1/η0.

We cover \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,N^{\alpha +1}] \times \mathbb {R}\times N^{\alpha +1} \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}[0,Nα+1]×R×Nα+1T with balls of size \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N^{\alpha +1}$$\end{document}Nα+1 and change to continuous approximation.

But the estimates for the measures of the inflated flat sets are clearly invariant under Galilean transforms, which establishes the general case. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 3.13

It seems possible to improve the estimate on frequency-dependent times, which is presently omitted to lighten the argument. We refer to [28, Section 3.5] for further details.

We record the following corollary, which will simplify the computations in the nonlinear analysis:

Corollary 3.14

Under the assumptions of Proposition 3.12, the following estimate holds:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert S_\alpha (t) f \Vert _{L_{t,x,y}^4([0,1], \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})} \lesssim _\varepsilon N^{\frac{2-\alpha }{8}+\varepsilon } k^{\frac{1}{12}} \Vert f \Vert _{L^2(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {T})}.\nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Sα(t)f‖Lt,x,y4([0,1],R×T)≲εN2-α8+εk112‖f‖L2(R×T).

Bilinear Strichartz Estimates

We formulate two simple bilinear Strichartz estimates hinging on first and second order transversality. We digress for a moment to explain bilinear Strichartz estimates, which hinges on the difference of the group velocities. This is referred to as first-order transversality. An early instance is due to Bourgain [8, Lemma 111] for solutions to the Schrödinger equation in two-dimensions:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert e^{it \Delta } f_1 e^{it \Delta } f_2 \Vert _{L^2_{t,x,y}(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {R}^2)} \lesssim \big ( \frac{N_2}{N_1} \big )^{\frac{1}{2}} \Vert f_1 \Vert _{L^2} \Vert f_2 \Vert _{L^2}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖eitΔf1eitΔf2‖Lt,x,y2(R×R2)≲(N2N1)12‖f1‖L2‖f2‖L2,with the frequency supports satisfying\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \text {supp}(\hat{f}_i) \subseteq \{ (\xi ,\eta ) \in \mathbb {R}^2 : |(\xi ,\eta )| \sim N_i \}, \quad N_2 \ll N_1. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supp(f^i)⊆{(ξ,η)∈R2:|(ξ,η)|∼Ni},N2≪N1.Formulated for functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_i \in L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {R}^2)$$\end{document}Fi∈Lτ,ξ,η2(R×R2), localized in frequencies and modulations, the above becomes a convolution estimate:23\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert F_1 * F_2 \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }} \lesssim \big ( \frac{N_2}{N_1} \big )^{\frac{1}{2}} \prod _{i=1}^2 L_i^{\frac{1}{2}} \Vert F_i \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖F1∗F2‖Lτ,ξ,η2≲(N2N1)12∏i=12Li12‖Fi‖Lτ,ξ,η2.This can be proved directly by overlap estimates for the supports, for which we can take advantage of the difference\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |\nabla \omega (\xi _1,\eta _1) - \nabla \omega (\xi _2,\eta _2) | \sim N_1, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|∇ω(ξ1,η1)-∇ω(ξ2,η2)|∼N1,with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _i,\eta _i) \in \text {supp}_{\xi ,\eta }(F_i)$$\end{document}(ξi,ηi)∈suppξ,η(Fi).

In the context of fractional KP-II equations we note that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |\nabla \omega _\alpha (\xi _1,\eta _1) - \nabla \omega _\alpha (\xi _2,\eta _2) | \gtrsim \big | \frac{\eta _1}{\xi _1} - \frac{\eta _2}{\xi _2} \big |. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|∇ωα(ξ1,η1)-∇ωα(ξ2,η2)|≳|η1ξ1-η2ξ2|.In the case where there is no lower bound on the difference, we might still take advantage of the growth of the difference, which is governed by the second derivative \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial ^2 \omega $$\end{document}∂2ω. In the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta $$\end{document}η-direction we find \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\partial _{\eta }^2 \omega (\xi _1,\eta _1) - \partial _{\eta }^2 \omega (\xi _2,\eta _2)| \sim \big | \frac{1}{\xi _1} - \frac{1}{\xi _2} \big |$$\end{document}|∂η2ω(ξ1,η1)-∂η2ω(ξ2,η2)|∼|1ξ1-1ξ2|. A lower bound on this difference will be referred to as second-order transversality.

The analog of (23) in the context of (1) on cylindrical domains reads as follows:

Lemma 4.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_i \in 2^{\mathbb {Z}}$$\end{document}Ni∈2Z, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_i \in 2^{\mathbb {N}_0}$$\end{document}Li∈2N0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_i \subseteq \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}Ii⊆R, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1,2$$\end{document}i=1,2 be two intervals with length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K \in 2^{\mathbb {Z}}$$\end{document}K∈2Z, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_{i,N_i,L_i}: \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {Z}\rightarrow \mathbb {R}_{\geqq 0}$$\end{document}fi,Ni,Li:R×R×Z→R≧0 with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {supp}(f_{i,N_i,L_i}) \subseteq \{ (\xi ,\eta ,\tau ): \xi \in I_i, \; | \xi | \sim N_i, \; |\tau - \omega _\alpha (\xi ,\eta )| \leqq L_i \}$$\end{document}supp(fi,Ni,Li)⊆{(ξ,η,τ):ξ∈Ii,|ξ|∼Ni,|τ-ωα(ξ,η)|≦Li}. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{\max } = \max (L_1,L_2)$$\end{document}Lmax=max(L1,L2), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{\min } = \min (L_1,L_2)$$\end{document}Lmin=min(L1,L2). Furthermore, we assume that24\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \big | \frac{\eta _1}{\xi _1} - \frac{\eta _2}{\xi _2} \big | \geqq D > 0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|η1ξ1-η2ξ2|≧D>0for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _i,\eta _i) \in \text {supp}_{\xi ,\eta }(f_{i,N_i,L_i})$$\end{document}(ξi,ηi)∈suppξ,η(fi,Ni,Li). Then the following estimate holds:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert f_{1,N_1,L_1} * f_{2,N_2,L_2} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }} \lesssim K^{\frac{1}{2}} L_{\min }^{\frac{1}{2}} \langle L_{\max } / D \rangle ^{\frac{1}{2}} \prod _{i=1}^2 \Vert f_{i,N_i,L_i} \Vert _{L^2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖f1,N1,L1∗f2,N2,L2‖Lτ,ξ,η2≲K12Lmin12⟨Lmax/D⟩12∏i=12‖fi,Ni,Li‖L2.

Proof

This is the analog of [28, Proposition 4.2] for functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_i: \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {Z}\rightarrow \mathbb {R}_{\geqq 0}$$\end{document}fi:R×R×Z→R≧0. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Next, we record a consequence of second-order transversality, which holds independently of (24). This will be very useful to estimate some limiting cases.

Lemma 4.2

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K, N_i, L_i, I_i, f_i: \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {Z}\rightarrow \mathbb {R}_{\geqq 0}$$\end{document}K,Ni,Li,Ii,fi:R×R×Z→R≧0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1,2$$\end{document}i=1,2 be like in Lemma 4.1. Then the following estimate holds:25\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert f_{1,N_1,L_1} * f_{2,N_2,L_2} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }} \lesssim K^{\frac{1}{2}} L_{\min }^{\frac{1}{2}} \langle L_{\max } N_{\min } \rangle ^{\frac{1}{4}} \prod _{i=1}^2 \Vert f_{i,N_i,L_i} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖f1,N1,L1∗f2,N2,L2‖Lτ,ξ,η2≲K12Lmin12⟨LmaxNmin⟩14∏i=12‖fi,Ni,Li‖Lτ,ξ,η2.

Proof

This the analog of [28, Proposition 4.3]. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

In the next proposition we combine the two estimates with almost-orthogonal decompositions in case of small transversality.

Proposition 4.3

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_i \in 2^{\mathbb {Z}}$$\end{document}Ni∈2Z, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_i \in 2^{\mathbb {N}_0}$$\end{document}Li∈2N0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1,2$$\end{document}i=1,2, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N_2 \ll N_1$$\end{document}N2≪N1. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_{i,N_i,L_i} \in L^2(\mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {R}\times \mathbb {Z})$$\end{document}fi,Ni,Li∈L2(R×R×Z) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {supp}(f_{i,N_i,L_i}) \subseteq D_{N_i,L_i}$$\end{document}supp(fi,Ni,Li)⊆DNi,Li, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1,2$$\end{document}i=1,2. Suppose that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \big | \frac{\eta _1}{\xi _1} - \frac{\eta _2}{\xi _2} \big | \leqq D^* \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|η1ξ1-η2ξ2|≦D∗for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _i,\eta _i) \in \text {supp}_{\xi ,\eta }(f_i)$$\end{document}(ξi,ηi)∈suppξ,η(fi), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1,2$$\end{document}i=1,2. Then the following estimate holds:26\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert f_{1,N_1,L_1} * f_{2,N_2,L_2} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }} \lesssim \log (D^*) N_2^{\frac{1}{2}} L_{\min }^{\frac{1}{2}} \langle L_{\max } / N_1^{\frac{\alpha }{2}} \rangle ^{\frac{1}{2}} \prod _{i=1}^2 \Vert f_{i,N_i,L_i} \Vert _{L^2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖f1,N1,L1∗f2,N2,L2‖Lτ,ξ,η2≲log(D∗)N212Lmin12⟨Lmax/N1α2⟩12∏i=12‖fi,Ni,Li‖L2.

Proof

First, suppose that we are in the case of large modulation: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{\max } \gtrsim N_1^{\alpha } N_2$$\end{document}Lmax≳N1αN2. In this case the estimate is immediate from Lemma 4.2.

In the following we suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{\max } \lesssim N_1^{\alpha } N_2$$\end{document}Lmax≲N1αN2. We carry out a Whitney decomposition\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert f_{1,N_1,L_1} * f_{2,N_2,L_2} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }} \leqq \sum _{\big ( \frac{L_{\max }}{N_2} \big )^{\frac{1}{2}} \leqq D \leqq D^* } \sum _{J_1 \sim _D J_2} \Vert f^{J_1}_{1,N_1,L_1} * f^{J_2}_{2,N_2,L_2} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖f1,N1,L1∗f2,N2,L2‖Lτ,ξ,η2≦∑(LmaxN2)12≦D≦D∗∑J1∼DJ2‖f1,N1,L11∗f2,N2,L22‖Lτ,ξ,η2.Above \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J_1$$\end{document}J1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J_2$$\end{document}J2 denote intervals of length D with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {dist}(J_1,J_2) \sim D$$\end{document}dist(J1,J2)∼D such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _i,\eta _i) \in \text {supp}_{\xi ,\eta }(f_{i,N_i,L_i})$$\end{document}(ξi,ηi)∈suppξ,η(fi,Ni,Li).

We note that by convolution constraint we have for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\xi _i,\eta _i,\tau _i), \, (\xi _{i+2}, \eta _{i+2},\tau _{i+2}) \in \text {supp}(f_{i,N_i,L_i})$$\end{document}(ξi,ηi,τi),(ξi+2,ηi+2,τi+2)∈supp(fi,Ni,Li), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1,2$$\end{document}i=1,2:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left\{ \begin{array}{cl} \xi _1 + \xi _2 & = \xi _3 + \xi _4, \\ \eta _1 + \eta _2 & = \eta _3 + \eta _4, \\ \xi _1^{\alpha +1} - \frac{\eta _1^2}{\xi _1} + \xi _2^{\alpha +1} - \frac{\eta _2^2}{\xi _2} & = \xi _3^{\alpha +1} - \frac{\eta _3^2}{\xi _3} + \xi _4^{\alpha +1} - \frac{\eta _4^2}{\xi _4} + \mathcal {O}(L_{\max }). \end{array} \right. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ξ1+ξ2=ξ3+ξ4,η1+η2=η3+η4,ξ1α+1-η12ξ1+ξ2α+1-η22ξ2=ξ3α+1-η32ξ3+ξ4α+1-η42ξ4+O(Lmax).We square the second line, divide by the first line and subtract the resulting expression from the third line to find:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left\{ \begin{array}{cl} \xi _1 + \xi _2 & = \xi _3 + \xi _4, \\ \xi _1^{\alpha +1} + \xi _2^{\alpha +1} - In the limiting case, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D = \big ( \frac{L_{\max }}{N_2} \big )^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}D=(LmaxN2)12, we find an almost orthogonal decomposition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _i'$$\end{document}ξi′ into intervals of length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{\max }/N_1^{\alpha +1}$$\end{document}Lmax/N1α+1. So, after inverting the scaling, we find that the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _i$$\end{document}ξi are decomposed into intervals \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _i$$\end{document}θi of length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{L_{\max }}{N_1^{\alpha }}$$\end{document}LmaxN1α. We write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sum _{J_1 \sim _D J_2} \Vert f^{J_1}_{1,N_1,L_1} * f^{J_2}_{2,N_2,L_2} \Vert _{L^2} \leqq \sum _{\begin{array}{c} J_1 \sim _D J_2, \\ \theta _1 \sim \theta _2 \end{array}} \Vert f^{J_1,\theta _1}_{1,N_1,L_1} * f^{J_2,\theta _2}_{2,N_2,L_2} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑J1∼DJ2‖f1,N1,L11∗f2,N2,L22‖L2≦∑J1∼DJ2,θ1∼θ2‖f1,N1,L1J1,θ1∗f2,N2,L2J2,θ2‖Lτ,ξ,η2.This expression is estimated by Lemma 4.2:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert f_{1,N_1,L_1}^{J_1,\theta _1} * f_{2,N_2,L_2}^{J_2,\theta _2} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }} \lesssim \big ( \frac{L_{\max }}{N_1^{\alpha +1}} \big )^{\frac{1}{2}} L_{\min }^{\frac{1}{2}} \langle L_{\max } N_2 \rangle ^{\frac{1}{4}} \prod _{i=1}^2 \Vert f_{i,N_i,L_i}^{J_i,\theta _i} \Vert _{L^2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖f1,N1,L1J1,θ1∗f2,N2,L2J2,θ2‖Lτ,ξ,η2≲(LmaxN1α+1)12Lmin12⟨LmaxN2⟩14∏i=12‖fi,Ni,LiJi,θi‖L2.The sum over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J_i$$\end{document}Ji and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\theta _i$$\end{document}θi can be carried out without loss by almost orthogonality. By the assumption \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_{\max } \lesssim N_1^\alpha N_2$$\end{document}Lmax≲N1αN2 this estimate suffices for (26).

We turn to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\big ( \frac{L_{\max }}{N_2} \big )^{\frac{1}{2}} < D \leqq N_1^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}$$\end{document}(LmaxN2)12<D≦N1α2. In this case, the identity\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left\{ \begin{array}{cl} \xi _1' + \xi _2' & = \xi _3' + \xi _4', \\ (\xi _1')^{\alpha +1} + (\xi _2')^{\alpha +1} & = (\xi _3')^{\alpha +1} + (\xi _4')^{\alpha +1} + \mathcal {O} \big ( \frac{D^2 N_2}{N_1^{\alpha +1}} \big ) \end{array} \right. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ξ1′+ξ2′=ξ3′+ξ4′,(ξ1′)α+1+(ξ2′)α+1=(ξ3′)α+1+(ξ4′)α+1+O(D2N2N1α+1)yields an almost orthogonal decomposition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _i'$$\end{document}ξi′ into intervals of length \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{D^2 N_2}{N_1^{\alpha +1}}$$\end{document}D2N2N1α+1 and after rescaling, we find a decomposition of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _i$$\end{document}ξi into intervals \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\quad \sum _{\big ( \frac{L_{\max }}{N_2} \big )^{\frac{1}{2}}< D \leqq N_1^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}} \sum _{\begin{array}{c} J_1 \sim _D J_2, \\ \theta _1 \sim \theta _2 \end{array}} \Vert f^{J_1,\theta _1}_{1,N_1,L_1} * f^{J_2,\theta _2}_{2,N_2,L_2} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }} \\&\lesssim \sum _{\big ( \frac{L_{\max }}{N_2} \big )^{\frac{1}{2}} < D \leqq N_1^{\frac{\alpha }{2}}} \sum _{\begin{array}{c} J_1 \sim _D J_2, \\ \theta _1 \sim \theta _2 \end{array}} \big }} \\&\lesssim L_{\min }^{\frac{1}{2}} N_2^{\frac{1}{2}} \langle L_{\max } / N_1^{\frac{\alpha }{2}} \rangle ^{\frac{1}{2}} \prod _{i=1}^2 \Vert f_{i,N_i,L_i} \Vert _{L^2_{\tau ,\xi ,\eta }}. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∑(LmaxN2)12<D≦N1α2∑J1∼DJ2‖f1,N1,L11∗f2,N2,L22‖L2≦∑(LmaxN2)12<D≦N1α2∑J1∼DJ2,θ1∼θ2‖f1,N1,L1J1,θ1∗f2,N2,L2J2,θ2‖Lτ,ξ,η2≲∑(LmaxN2)12<D≦N1α2∑J1∼DJ2,θ1∼θ2(D2N2N1α)12Lmin12⟨Lmax/D⟩12∏i=12‖fi,Ni,LiJi,θi‖Lτ,ξ,η2≲Lmin12N212⟨Lmax/N1α2⟩12∏i=12‖fi,Ni,Li‖Lτ,ξ,η2.Finally, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D \in [N_1^{\frac{\alpha }{2}},D^*]$$\end{document}D∈[N1α2,D∗] we carry out a simple almost orthogonal decomposition of
